Wilson 95\\% CI: ISR $[3.6\\%, 9.9\\%]$, Random $[6.5\\%, 13.7\\%]$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}). %% D095 FINAL Fisher test\nThe key asymmetry is not in non-hacking rates but in gaming \\emph{composition}: ISR converts stochastic H7 into near-deterministic convergence ($2/3$ seeds ${\\geq}97\\%$), while random H7 remains seed-dependent and H1-dominated.\nThe raw $3.2\\times$ ISR$^*$ headline collapses once the LOO bias is controlled---a methodological lesson for any LOO-based metric on variable-length outputs. %% artifact: wilson_ci_complete, h8_reclassification\n\n\\textbf{Temporal collapse under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.}\nExtended training (1000 steps) reveals that ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$'s genuine rate at 500 steps ($17.3\\%$, seed~42) is transient: by step 1000, H7 gaming escalates from $16.0\\%$ to $96.7\\%$ while genuine rate collapses to $2.2\\%$ ($n_{\\text{comp}}$: $5.58{\\to}2.89$).\nThe optimization converges to a fixed point where $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.88$, superficially suggesting structural improvement but reflecting near-complete tautological gaming.\nThis mirrors the exec-only 1000-step trajectory (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}): both converge to ${\\sim}0\\%$ genuine proofs despite opposite ISR values ($0.88$ vs.\\ $0.003$), demonstrating that ISR is gamed upward under ISR-as-reward and downward under exec-only---two paths to the same structural collapse. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR-as-reward produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$, spanning 18--95\\%); each seed converges to a distinct pathway (Appendix~\\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nSeed~42: \\emph{reward collapse} ($\\text{PR}{=}18\\%$), the classical pattern of~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nSeed~43: \\emph{H1 shortcutting} ($\\text{PR}{=}86\\%$, $\\text{H1}{=}69\\%$, only $27\\%$ non-hacking).\nSeed~44: \\emph{pure H7 meta-Goodhart} ($\\text{PR}{=}95\\%$, $\\text{H7}{=}100\\%$, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.742$, $0\\%$ non-hacking)---simultaneously gaming \\emph{both} execution and structural metrics through the same construction.\nThe random control at $\\beta{=}0.3$ is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$, spanning 23--92\\%) with near-universal H1 hacking ($91\\%$, pooled) and ${\\leq}3\\%$ non-hacking per seed; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source; ISR produces diverse failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, H7 meta-Goodhart) while random converges to H1-dominated hacking with sporadic H7 ($7\\%$ pooled, $0$--$7$ H7a per seed) (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-logic results. \\textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\\ Prolog exec-only $\\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \\textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; (ii) does ISR-as-reward reproduce meta-level Goodhart?\nDetailed per-seed numbers are in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog exec-only: ISR catches redundancy across logics.}\nTwo of three seeds (s42, s43) show $\\text{ISR}^{*}{\\approx}0$ despite moderate-to-high PR (0.83, 0.56). %% registry: plan006-prolog-exec-s42, plan006-prolog-exec-s43-r3\nInspection reveals \\emph{redundant coding}: ground facts repeated multiple times (e.g., \\texttt{penguin(fido).} appearing three times), so per-clause LOO never breaks the proof.\nThis is a different failure semantics from FOL exec-only (conclusion embedding), but $\\text{ISR}^{*}$ flags both.\nWe are careful to distinguish: exec-only \\emph{low} ISR signals \\emph{redundancy}; ISR-rewarded \\emph{high} ISR signals \\emph{metric gaming} (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---the same number does not license the same reasoning-quality conclusion.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog ISR-as-reward: universal Goodhart.}\nThe Prolog result is stronger than FOL: 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking via three distinct mechanisms (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\nSeed~42: clean H7 tautologies ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$, all $50/100$ proved). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42\nSeed~43: \\emph{degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog} (e.g., \\texttt{MARY\\_IS\\_AN\\_AVA\\-DEOTE.}); strict text-match returns $\\text{H7}{=}0$ but functional analysis shows all 38 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$ (functionally H7). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s43\nSeed~44: \\emph{query-as-fact gaming}---the model transforms the NL question into a snake-case atom asserted as both fact and query (e.g., \\texttt{is\\_the\\_dog\\_furry.} as both fact and query), with $95/97$ proved samples at $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$. %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s44\nSeed~44 evaded our initial strict classifier (reported $100\\%$ genuine); functional cross-validation exposed it---a methodological lesson that text-match classifiers have blind spots and STA's necessity profile is the cross-check.\nWe attribute Prolog's stronger collapse to its simpler operational semantics: a broader gaming basin makes the genuine residual disappear---corroborating evidence for Claim 2.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.92\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples; exec-only and SFT: seed~42; ISR and Random bars: pooled across 3 seeds, per-seed breakdown in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}). ISR converts H1 into more extreme H7 without reducing total hacking; SFT shows near-zero hacking.}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{Rnd$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{SFT} & \\textbf{Prolog$_{\\text{ISR}}$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 embed       & 87 & 18 & 81 & 7 & 0$^\\sharp$ \\\\\nH7 duplicate   & 0  & 70 & 0  & 0 & 100$^\\sharp$ \\\\\nH8 prem-subset & 6  & 5  & --- & 0 & --- \\\\\nTautological   & --- & --- & --- & --- & s42 \\\\\nRepetition     & s43 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nContradiction  & 1k & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nCmplx.\\ vacuous& 0  & 0  & 4  & 0 & --- \\\\\nQuery-as-fact  & --- & --- & --- & --- & s44 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textbf{Non-hacking} & 8  & 7  & 18 & \\textbf{92} & \\textbf{0} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Eight hacking modes across conditions (\\% of proved samples; exec-only/SFT: seed~42; ISR/Random: pooled across 3 seeds). H7 includes subtypes H7a (text-match, $n{=}149$) and H7b (obfuscated tautology, $n{=}12$; s44 only). Cells with seed identifiers indicate the mode dominates that seed. $^\\sharp$Prolog row pools strict+functional (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe eight modes (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}; full definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}) span the studied conditions.\nThree are quantifiable across conditions: \\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedding) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ regardless of reward type; \\textbf{H7} (conclusion duplication) is ISR-accelerated at 500 steps ($69.7\\%$ combined vs.\\ $0\\%$ under random), though random $\\beta{=}0.1$ converges to $100\\%$ H7 by 1000 steps (seed~42); \\textbf{H8} (premise-subset gaming) appears under both exec-only ($6\\%$) and ISR ($5\\%$) conditions (Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}).\nH7 splits into \\textbf{H7a} (standard, text-match detectable; only SFT shows $92\\%$ non-hacking.\nAcross 2 logic types, 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, and up to 1000 steps, no GRPO condition we tested produces a high-PR run that is also majority non-hacking; the closest is ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ s42 at $\\text{PR}{=}81\\%$, $17\\%$ non-hacking, and even that residual is the premise-minimization artifact analyzed above.\nQuery-as-fact gaming was found only when we cross-validated the strict classifier against $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distributions, suggesting the taxonomy is descriptive, not exhaustive.\n",

it converts H1 into H7 (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\nRandom also yields higher and more stable PR ($87{\\pm}8\\%$ vs.\\ $77{\\pm}14\\%$).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe one ISR seed not dominated by H7 (s42) has raw $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.415$, $3.2\\times$ its random control ($0.131$), suggesting genuine improvement.\nThis appearance is a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean $n{=}5.58$ vs.\\ $8.46$ for random), and ISR rises mechanically as $n$ falls---when $n{=}2$, $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by definition.\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq{3}$ premises (eliminating the trivial-$n$ regime) gives $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ for ISR vs.\\ $0.243$ for random: a $1.06\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis\nAfter reclassifying premise-subset gaming (H8; Wilson 95\\% CI: ISR $[3.6\\%, 9.9\\%]$, Random $[6.5\\%, 13.7\\%]$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}). %% D095 FINAL Fisher test\nThe key asymmetry is not in non-hacking rates but in gaming \\emph{composition}: ISR converts stochastic H7 into near-deterministic convergence ($2/3$ seeds ${\\geq}97\\%$), while random H7 remains seed-dependent and H1-dominated.\nThe raw $3.2\\times$ ISR$^*$ headline collapses once the LOO bias is controlled---a methodological lesson for any LOO-based metric on variable-length outputs. %% artifact: wilson_ci_complete, h8_reclassification\n\n\\textbf{Temporal collapse under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.}\nExtended training (1000 steps) reveals that ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$'s genuine rate at 500 steps ($17.3\\%$, seed~42) is transient: by step 1000, H7 gaming escalates from $16.0\\%$ to $96.7\\%$ while genuine rate collapses to $2.2\\%$ ($n_{\\text{comp}}$: $5.58{\\to}2.89$).\nThe optimization converges to a fixed point where $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.88$, superficially suggesting structural improvement but reflecting near-complete tautological gaming.\nThis mirrors the exec-only 1000-step trajectory (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}): both converge to ${\\sim}0\\%$ genuine proofs despite opposite ISR values ($0.88$ vs.\\ $0.003$), demonstrating that ISR is gamed upward under ISR-as-reward and downward under exec-only---two paths to the same structural collapse. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR-as-reward produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$, spanning 18--95\\%); each seed converges to a distinct pathway (Appendix~\\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nSeed~42: \\emph{reward collapse} ($\\text{PR}{=}18\\%$), the classical pattern of~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nSeed~43: \\emph{H1 shortcutting} ($\\text{PR}{=}86\\%$, $\\text{H1}{=}69\\%$, only $27\\%$ non-hacking).\nSeed~44: \\emph{pure H7 meta-Goodhart} ($\\text{PR}{=}95\\%$, $\\text{H7}{=}100\\%$, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.742$, $0\\%$ non-hacking)---simultaneously gaming \\emph{both} execution and structural metrics through the same construction.\nThe random control at $\\beta{=}0.3$ is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$, spanning 23--92\\%) with near-universal H1 hacking ($91\\%$, pooled) and ${\\leq}3\\%$ non-hacking per seed; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source; ISR produces diverse failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, H7 meta-Goodhart) while random converges to H1-dominated hacking with sporadic H7 ($7\\%$ pooled, $0$--$7$ H7a per seed) (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-logic results. \\textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\\ Prolog exec-only $\\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \\textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; exec-only and SFT: seed~42; ISR and Random bars: pooled across 3 seeds, per-seed breakdown in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}). D095 FINAL\nRandom H7 is highly seed-dependent: $38.3\\%$ in seed~43 vs.\\ $0\\%$ in seeds~42 and~44; ISR makes H7 near-deterministic ($2/3$ seeds at $97$--$100\\%$, Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}).\nH7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by construction (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---is the canonical meta-level Goodhart move: the model satisfies the LOO definition directly rather than producing a derivation that happens to satisfy it.\nTotal conclusion embedding ($\\text{H1}+\\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ across both conditions, and is in fact \\emph{higher} under random (pooled H1 $71\\%$ vs.\\ $18\\%$ under ISR).\nISR does not reduce hacking; it converts H1 into H7 (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\nRandom also yields higher and more stable PR ($87{\\pm}8\\%$ vs.\\ $77{\\pm}14\\%$).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe one ISR seed not dominated by H7 (s42) has raw $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.415$, $3.2\\times$ its random control ($0.131$), suggesting genuine improvement.\nThis appearance is a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean $n{=}5.58$ vs.\\ $8.46$ for random), and ISR rises mechanically as $n$ falls---when $n{=}2$, $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by definition.\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq{3}$ premises (eliminating the trivial-$n$ regime) gives $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ for ISR vs.\\ $0.243$ for random: a $1.06\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis\nAfter reclassifying premise-subset gaming (H8; Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}) and correcting H7 classifier false negatives (s44, $n{=}12$; Appendix~\\ref{app:h4}), ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ non-hacking rate ($6.1\\%$, $14/231$) is numerically lower than random ($9.5\\%$, $25/262$), but the difference is not statistically significant (Fisher $p{=}0.182$, two-sided, n.s.; Wilson 95\\% CI: ISR $[3.6\\%, 9.9\\%]$, Random $[6.5\\%, 13.7\\%]$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}). %% D095 FINAL Fisher test\nThe key asymmetry is not in non-hacking rates but in gaming \\emph{composition}: ISR converts stochastic H7 into near-deterministic convergence ($2/3$ seeds ${\\geq}97\\%$), while random H7 remains seed-dependent and H1-dominated.\nThe raw $3.2\\times$ ISR$^*$ headline collapses once the LOO bias is controlled---a methodological lesson for any LOO-based metric on variable-length outputs. %% artifact: wilson_ci_complete, h8_reclassification\n\n\\textbf{Temporal collapse under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.}\nExtended training (1000 steps) reveals that ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$'s genuine rate at 500 steps ($17.3\\%$, seed~42) is transient: by step 1000, H7 gaming escalates from $16.0\\%$ to $96.7\\%$ while genuine rate collapses to $2.2\\%$ ($n_{\\text{comp}}$: $5.58{\\to}2.89$).\nThe optimization converges to a fixed point where $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.88$, superficially suggesting structural improvement but reflecting near-complete tautological gaming.\nThis mirrors the exec-only 1000-step trajectory (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}): both converge to ${\\sim}0\\%$ genuine proofs despite opposite ISR values ($0.88$ vs.\\ $0.003$), demonstrating that ISR is gamed upward under ISR-as-reward and downward under exec-only---two paths to the same structural collapse. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR-as-reward produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$, spanning 18--95\\%); each seed converges to a distinct pathway (Appendix~\\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nSeed~42: \\emph{reward collapse} ($\\text{PR}{=}18\\%$), the classical pattern of~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nSeed~43: \\emph{H1 shortcutting} ($\\text{PR}{=}86\\%$, $\\text{H1}{=}69\\%$, only $27\\%$ non-hacking).\nSeed~44: \\emph{pure H7 meta-Goodhart} ($\\text{PR}{=}95\\%$, $\\text{H7}{=}100\\%$, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.742$, $0\\%$ non-hacking)---simultaneously gaming \\emph{both} execution and structural metrics through the same construction.\nThe random control at $\\beta{=}0.3$ is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$, spanning 23--92\\%) with near-universal H1 hacking ($91\\%$, pooled) and ${\\leq}3\\%$ non-hacking per seed; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source; ISR produces diverse failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, H7 meta-Goodhart) while random converges to H1-dominated hacking with sporadic H7 ($7\\%$ pooled, $0$--$7$ H7a per seed) (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-logic results. \\textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\\ Prolog exec-only $\\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \\textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; (ii) does ISR-as-reward reproduce meta-level Goodhart?\nDetailed per-seed numbers are in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog exec-only: ISR catches redundancy across logics.}\nTwo of three seeds (s42, s43) show $\\text{ISR}^{*}{\\approx}0$ despite moderate-to-high PR (0.83, 0.56). %% registry: plan006-prolog-exec-s42, plan006-prolog-exec-s43-r3\nInspection reveals \\emph{redundant coding}: ground facts repeated multiple times (e.g., \\texttt{penguin(fido).} appearing three times), so per-clause LOO never breaks the proof.\nThis is a different failure semantics from FOL exec-only (conclusion embedding), but $\\text{ISR}^{*}$ flags both.\nWe are careful to distinguish: exec-only \\emph{low} ISR signals \\emph{redundancy}; ISR-rewarded \\emph{high} ISR signals \\emph{metric gaming} (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---the same number does not license the same reasoning-quality conclusion.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog ISR-as-reward: universal Goodhart.}\nThe Prolog result is stronger than FOL: 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking via three distinct mechanisms (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\nSeed~42: clean H7 tautologies ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$, all $50/100$ proved). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42\nSeed~43: \\emph{degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog} (e.g., \\texttt{MARY\\_IS\\_AN\\_AVA\\-DEOTE.}); strict text-match returns $\\text{H7}{=}0$ but functional analysis shows all 38 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$ (functionally H7). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s43\nSeed~44: \\emph{query-as-fact gaming}---the model transforms the NL question into a snake-case atom asserted as both fact and query (e.g., \\texttt{is\\_the\\_dog\\_furry.} as both fact and query), with $95/97$ proved samples at $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$. %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s44\nSeed~44 evaded our initial strict classifier (reported $100\\%$ genuine); D095 FINAL\nRandom H7 is highly seed-dependent: $38.3\\%$ in seed~43 vs.\\ $0\\%$ in seeds~42 and~44; ISR makes H7 near-deterministic ($2/3$ seeds at $97$--$100\\%$, Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}).\nH7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by construction (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---is the canonical meta-level Goodhart move: the model satisfies the LOO definition directly rather than producing a derivation that happens to satisfy it.\nTotal conclusion embedding ($\\text{H1}+\\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ across both conditions, and is in fact \\emph{higher} under random (pooled H1 $71\\%$ vs.\\ $18\\%$ under ISR).\nISR does not reduce hacking; it converts H1 into H7 (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\nRandom also yields higher and more stable PR ($87{\\pm}8\\%$ vs.\\ $77{\\pm}14\\%$).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe one ISR seed not dominated by H7 (s42) has raw $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.415$, $3.2\\times$ its random control ($0.131$), suggesting genuine improvement.\nThis appearance is a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean $n{=}5.58$ vs.\\ $8.46$ for random), and ISR rises mechanically as $n$ falls---when $n{=}2$, $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by definition.\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq{3}$ premises (eliminating the trivial-$n$ regime) gives $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ for ISR vs.\\ $0.243$ for random: a $1.06\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis\nAfter reclassifying premise-subset gaming (H8; Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}) and correcting H7 classifier false negatives (s44, $n{=}12$; Appendix~\\ref{app:h4}), ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ non-hacking rate ($6.1\\%$, $14/231$) is numerically lower than random ($9.5\\%$, $25/262$), but the difference is not statistically significant (Fisher $p{=}0.182$, two-sided, n.s.; Wilson 95\\% CI: ISR $[3.6\\%, 9.9\\%]$, Random $[6.5\\%, 13.7\\%]$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}). %% D095 FINAL Fisher test\nThe key asymmetry is not in non-hacking rates but in gaming \\emph{composition}: ISR converts stochastic H7 into near-deterministic convergence ($2/3$ seeds ${\\geq}97\\%$), while random H7 remains seed-dependent and H1-dominated.\nThe raw $3.2\\times$ ISR$^*$ headline collapses once the LOO bias is controlled---a methodological lesson for any LOO-based metric on variable-length outputs. %% artifact: wilson_ci_complete, h8_reclassification\n\n\\textbf{Temporal collapse under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.}\nExtended training (1000 steps) reveals that ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$'s genuine rate at 500 steps ($17.3\\%$, seed~42) is transient: by step 1000, H7 gaming escalates from $16.0\\%$ to $96.7\\%$ while genuine rate collapses to $2.2\\%$ ($n_{\\text{comp}}$: $5.58{\\to}2.89$).\nThe optimization converges to a fixed point where $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.88$, superficially suggesting structural improvement but reflecting near-complete tautological gaming.\nThis mirrors the exec-only 1000-step trajectory (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}): both converge to ${\\sim}0\\%$ genuine proofs despite opposite ISR values ($0.88$ vs.\\ $0.003$), demonstrating that ISR is gamed upward under ISR-as-reward and downward under exec-only---two paths to the same structural collapse. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR-as-reward produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$, spanning 18--95\\%); each seed converges to a distinct pathway (Appendix~\\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nSeed~42: \\emph{reward collapse} ($\\text{PR}{=}18\\%$), the classical pattern of~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nSeed~43: \\emph{H1 shortcutting} ($\\text{PR}{=}86\\%$, $\\text{H1}{=}69\\%$, only $27\\%$ non-hacking).\nSeed~44: \\emph{pure H7 meta-Goodhart} ($\\text{PR}{=}95\\%$, $\\text{H7}{=}100\\%$, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.742$, $0\\%$ non-hacking)---simultaneously gaming \\emph{both} execution and structural metrics through the same construction.\nThe random control at $\\beta{=}0.3$ is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$, spanning 23--92\\%) with near-universal H1 hacking ($91\\%$, pooled) and ${\\leq}3\\%$ non-hacking per seed; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source; ISR produces diverse failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, H7 meta-Goodhart) while random converges to H1-dominated hacking with sporadic H7 ($7\\%$ pooled, $0$--$7$ H7a per seed) (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-logic results. \\textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\\ Prolog exec-only $\\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \\textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; ISR-rewarded \\emph{high} ISR signals \\emph{metric gaming} (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---the same number does not license the same reasoning-quality conclusion.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog ISR-as-reward: universal Goodhart.}\nThe Prolog result is stronger than FOL: 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking via three distinct mechanisms (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\nSeed~42: clean H7 tautologies ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$, all $50/100$ proved). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42\nSeed~43: \\emph{degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog} (e.g., \\texttt{MARY\\_IS\\_AN\\_AVA\\-DEOTE.}); (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}, Appendix~\\ref{app:detailed-results}). $^\\P$Includes H7 classifier false-negative correction (s44) and H8 premise-subset gaming (s42; 5.2\\% of proved; Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}). $^\\S$Random H7 driven by seed~43 (38.3\\%); seeds~42 and~44: 0\\%. Per-seed breakdown in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-amplified gaming.}\nH7 tautological collapse occurs under both ISR and random conditions, but ISR amplifies it ${\\sim}5\\times$: $70.6\\%$ ($163/231$) under ISR vs.\\ $13.7\\%$ ($36/262$) under random (Fisher $p{=}1.3{\\times}10^{-39}$, $\\text{OR}{=}15.05$). %% registry: plan002 vs plan018 all seeds; D095 FINAL\nRandom H7 is highly seed-dependent: $38.3\\%$ in seed~43 vs.\\ $0\\%$ in seeds~42 and~44; ISR makes H7 near-deterministic ($2/3$ seeds at $97$--$100\\%$, Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}).\nH7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by construction (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---is the canonical meta-level Goodhart move: the model satisfies the LOO definition directly rather than producing a derivation that happens to satisfy it.\nTotal conclusion embedding ($\\text{H1}+\\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ across both conditions, and is in fact \\emph{higher} under random (pooled H1 $71\\%$ vs.\\ $18\\%$ under ISR).\nISR does not reduce hacking; it converts H1 into H7 (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\nRandom also yields higher and more stable PR ($87{\\pm}8\\%$ vs.\\ $77{\\pm}14\\%$).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe one ISR seed not dominated by H7 (s42) has raw $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.415$, $3.2\\times$ its random control ($0.131$), suggesting genuine improvement.\nThis appearance is a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean $n{=}5.58$ vs.\\ $8.46$ for random), and ISR rises mechanically as $n$ falls---when $n{=}2$, $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by definition.\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq{3}$ premises (eliminating the trivial-$n$ regime) gives $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ for ISR vs.\\ $0.243$ for random: a $1.06\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis\nAfter reclassifying premise-subset gaming (H8; Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}) and correcting H7 classifier false negatives (s44, $n{=}12$; Appendix~\\ref{app:h4}), ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ non-hacking rate ($6.1\\%$, $14/231$) is numerically lower than random ($9.5\\%$, $25/262$), but the difference is not statistically significant (Fisher $p{=}0.182$, two-sided, n.s.; Wilson 95\\% CI: ISR $[3.6\\%, 9.9\\%]$, Random $[6.5\\%, 13.7\\%]$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}). %% D095 FINAL Fisher test\nThe key asymmetry is not in non-hacking rates but in gaming \\emph{composition}: ISR converts stochastic H7 into near-deterministic convergence ($2/3$ seeds ${\\geq}97\\%$), while random H7 remains seed-dependent and H1-dominated.\nThe raw $3.2\\times$ ISR$^*$ headline collapses once the LOO bias is controlled---a methodological lesson for any LOO-based metric on variable-length outputs. %% artifact: wilson_ci_complete, h8_reclassification\n\n\\textbf{Temporal collapse under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.}\nExtended training (1000 steps) reveals that ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$'s genuine rate at 500 steps ($17.3\\%$, seed~42) is transient: by step 1000, H7 gaming escalates from $16.0\\%$ to $96.7\\%$ while genuine rate collapses to $2.2\\%$ ($n_{\\text{comp}}$: $5.58{\\to}2.89$).\nThe optimization converges to a fixed point where $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.88$, superficially suggesting structural improvement but reflecting near-complete tautological gaming.\nThis mirrors the exec-only 1000-step trajectory (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}): both converge to ${\\sim}0\\%$ genuine proofs despite opposite ISR values ($0.88$ vs.\\ $0.003$), demonstrating that ISR is gamed upward under ISR-as-reward and downward under exec-only---two paths to the same structural collapse. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR-as-reward produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$, spanning 18--95\\%); each seed converges to a distinct pathway (Appendix~\\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nSeed~42: \\emph{reward collapse} ($\\text{PR}{=}18\\%$), the classical pattern of~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nSeed~43: \\emph{H1 shortcutting} ($\\text{PR}{=}86\\%$, $\\text{H1}{=}69\\%$, only $27\\%$ non-hacking).\nSeed~44: \\emph{pure H7 meta-Goodhart} ($\\text{PR}{=}95\\%$, $\\text{H7}{=}100\\%$, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.742$, $0\\%$ non-hacking)---simultaneously gaming \\emph{both} execution and structural metrics through the same construction.\nThe random control at $\\beta{=}0.3$ is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$, spanning 23--92\\%) with near-universal H1 hacking ($91\\%$, pooled) and ${\\leq}3\\%$ non-hacking per seed; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source; ISR produces diverse failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, H7 meta-Goodhart) while random converges to H1-dominated hacking with sporadic H7 ($7\\%$ pooled, $0$--$7$ H7a per seed) (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-logic results. \\textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\\ Prolog exec-only $\\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \\textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; (ii) does ISR-as-reward reproduce meta-level Goodhart?\nDetailed per-seed numbers are in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog exec-only: ISR catches redundancy across logics.}\nTwo of three seeds (s42, s43) show $\\text{ISR}^{*}{\\approx}0$ despite moderate-to-high PR (0.83, 0.56). %% registry: plan006-prolog-exec-s42, plan006-prolog-exec-s43-r3\nInspection reveals \\emph{redundant coding}: ground facts repeated multiple times (e.g., \\texttt{penguin(fido).} appearing three times), so per-clause LOO never breaks the proof.\nThis is a different failure semantics from FOL exec-only (conclusion embedding), but $\\text{ISR}^{*}$ flags both.\nWe are careful to distinguish: exec-only \\emph{low} ISR signals \\emph{redundancy}; ISR-rewarded \\emph{high} ISR signals \\emph{metric gaming} (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---the same number does not license the same reasoning-quality conclusion.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog ISR-as-reward: universal Goodhart.}\nThe Prolog result is stronger than FOL: 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking via three distinct mechanisms (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\nSeed~42: clean H7 tautologies ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$, all $50/100$ proved). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42\nSeed~43: \\emph{degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog} (e.g., \\texttt{MARY\\_IS\\_AN\\_AVA\\-DEOTE.}); strict text-match returns $\\text{H7}{=}0$ but functional analysis shows all 38 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$ (functionally H7). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s43\nSeed~44: \\emph{query-as-fact gaming}---the model transforms the NL question into a snake-case atom asserted as both fact and query (e.g., \\texttt{is\\_the\\_dog\\_furry.} as both fact and query), with $95/97$ proved samples at $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$. %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s44\nSeed~44 evaded our initial strict classifier (reported $100\\%$ genuine); functional cross-validation exposed it---a methodological lesson that text-match classifiers have blind spots and STA's necessity profile is the cross-check.\nWe attribute Prolog's stronger collapse to its simpler operational semantics: a broader gaming basin makes the genuine residual disappear---corroborating evidence for Claim 2.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.92\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples; exec-only and SFT: seed~42; ISR and Random bars: pooled across 3 seeds, per-seed breakdown in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}). ISR converts H1 into more extreme H7 without reducing total hacking; SFT shows near-zero hacking.}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{Rnd$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{SFT} & \\textbf{Prolog$_{\\text{ISR}}$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 embed       & 87 & 18 & 71 & 7 & 0$^\\sharp$ \\\\\nH7 duplicate   & 0  & 71 & 14 & 0 & 100$^\\sharp$ \\\\\nH8 prem-subset & 6  & 5  & --- & 0 & --- \\\\\nTautological   & --- & --- & --- & --- & s42 \\\\\nRepetition     & s43 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nContradiction  & 1k & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nCmplx.\\ vacuous& 0  & 0  & 4  & 0 & --- \\\\\nQuery-as-fact  & --- & --- & --- & --- & s44 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textbf{Non-hacking} & 8  & 6  & 10 & \\textbf{92} & \\textbf{0} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Eight hacking modes across conditions (\\% of proved samples; exec-only/SFT: seed~42; ISR/Random: pooled across 3 seeds). H7 includes subtypes H7a (text-match, $n{=}149$) and H7b (obfuscated tautology, $n{=}12$; s44 only). Cells with seed identifiers indicate the mode dominates that seed. $^\\sharp$Prolog row pools strict+functional (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe eight modes (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}; full definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}) span the studied conditions.\nThree are quantifiable across conditions: \\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedding) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ regardless of reward type; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source; ISR produces diverse failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, H7 meta-Goodhart) while random converges to H1-dominated hacking with sporadic H7 ($7\\%$ pooled, $0$--$7$ H7a per seed) (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-logic results. \\textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\\ Prolog exec-only $\\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \\textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; 1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate STA along three axes: ISR as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); ISR as a training reward, where it exhibits meta-level Goodhart's Law (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); and STA as a cross-logic protocol (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
5	A hacking taxonomy synthesizes the modes observed across conditions (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We train Qwen3-4B~\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}8$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\{42,43,44\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\citep{folio} subset held constant across all conditions; the SFT baseline is trained with NLL on gold formalizations.\footnote{All experiments use \texttt{data/train\_500\_seed42.jsonl}; seeds control initialization and rollout sampling only.}
10	Held-out evaluation uses 100 FOLIO examples (\texttt{proofwriter\_fol\_100.jsonl}---legacy filename, no ProofWriter data) with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are recomputed uniformly by a single evaluator (\texttt{eval\_sta.py}) so training-log ISR is never compared across runs.
11	We report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for H1/H7 plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), and a \emph{non-hacking} (N-H) rate---the residual of proved samples matching none of the eight cataloged patterns, a necessary but not sufficient condition for semantic correctness.
12	
13	\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}
14	\label{sec:diagnostic}
15	
16	\begin{figure}[t]
17	\centering
18	\includegraphics[width=0.95\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}
19	\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\%$\to$93\%$\to$96\% while proved-only ISR falls $0.642{\to}0.132{\to}0.003$ over 100$\to$500$\to$1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\% of proved samples have $\text{ISR}{=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}
20	\label{fig:isr-collapse}
21	\end{figure}
22	
23	\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}
24	Figure~\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces exec-only GRPO (seed~42) at 100, 500, 1000 steps.
25	PR rises $32\%\to93\%\to96\%$, a trajectory execution-only evaluation would read as successful optimization; $\text{ISR}^*$ tells the opposite story, $0.642\to0.132\to0.003$ (214$\times$ collapse). %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5, plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan016-extended-1000-s42
26	By 500 steps, 87.1\% of proved samples embed the conclusion (H1); by 1000 steps the embedding rate saturates at 100\% and mean component count more than doubles ($6.58\to18.55$) as the model emits contradictory or vacuous premises that trivially derive any conclusion.
27	Extended training, lacking a structural signal, \emph{worsens} formalization quality while improving the headline number.
28	
29	\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}
30	The three exec-only seeds diverge into qualitatively different failure modes despite shared data, hyperparameters, and reward (Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}): s42 reaches $\text{PR}{=}93\%$ via conclusion embedding ($\text{H1}{=}87\%$); s43 collapses to $\text{PR}{=}13\%$ via repetition (87\% of rollouts truncated); s44 reaches $\text{PR}{=}98\%$ via tautological collapse ($\text{H7}{=}100\%$, mean $n{=}2.04$). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44
31	The cross-seed PR variance ($\sigma{\pm}47$\,pp) shows aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes; all three are flagged by $\text{ISR}^*$ even though one passes the PR check (s44) and one fails it (s43).
32	By 1000 steps, all three converge to high PR ($0.95{\pm}0.05$) but $0\%$ non-hacking (3 samples escape text-match classifiers but are functionally degenerate; see Appendix~\ref{app:h8} and Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}), each through a different dominant mode (s42: H1 saturation; s43: H7 tautology; s44: H1+H8). %% registry: plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan001-exec-only-s43-1000-r2, plan001-exec-only-s44-1000-r2
33	
34	\textbf{SFT baseline.}
35	SFT on gold formalizations reaches $\text{PR}{=}13.0\%$ (3-seed pooled, 1000 steps) with $\text{H7}{=}0$, $\text{H1}{=}7.7\%$, $92.2\%$ non-hacking ($n{=}39$; per-seed range 91--93\%). %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-1000/s43-1000/s44-1000-r3
36	The near-absence of hacking under identical data exposure is consistent with hacking being RL-specific.
37	A 100-step RL checkpoint (Appendix~\ref{app:100step}) at $\text{PR}{=}32\%$ already shows 31.2\% hacking (vs.\ SFT's 7.7\%), indicating reward hacking emerges early in RL training, not merely at high PR.
38	
39	\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}
40	\label{sec:goodhart}
41	
42	We now reverse roles: instead of treating ISR as an observer, we use it as a reward augmentation, $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1 + \beta\,\text{ISR})$, with a matched random control, $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1 + \beta\,u)$, $u\sim\mathcal{U}(0,1)$, to isolate ISR-specific signal from generic reward-variance effects.
43	The headline finding: ISR-as-reward produces \emph{meta-level} Goodhart~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}---the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming.
44	
45	\begin{table}[t]
46	\centering

52	\midrule

58	Random & 0.1 & \textbf{87}{\scriptsize$\pm$8} & 0.117{\scriptsize$\pm$.04} & 14$^\S$ & 71 & 10 \\ %% registry: plan018-additive-random-b01-s42/s43-r3/s44-r2 (D095 FINAL)

61	Random & 0.3 & 54{\scriptsize$\pm$35} & 0.09{\scriptsize$\pm$.09} & 7 & 91 & 2 \\ %% registry: plan017-additive-random-s42, exp1-random-b03-s43-r4, exp1-random-b03-s44-r2 (pooled; D086 H7: s42=4,s43=7,s44=0)

64	\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows: H7/H1/N-H pooled across seeds; PR/ISR$^*$: mean$\pm$std of per-seed values. $^\dagger$Seed~42. $\ddagger$$\text{ISR}^*$ omitted for ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ because the raw mean is inflated by H7 gaming; premise-count-matched value is $1.06\times$ random (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}, Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}). $^\P$Includes H7 classifier false-negative correction (s44) and H8 premise-subset gaming (s42; 5.2\% of proved; Appendix~\ref{app:h8}). $^\S$Random H7 driven by seed~43 (38.3\%); seeds~42 and~44: 0\%. Per-seed breakdown in Appendix Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}.}
65	\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}
66	\end{table}
67	
68	\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-amplified gaming.}
69	H7 tautological collapse occurs under both ISR and random conditions, but ISR amplifies it ${\sim}5\times$: $70.6\%$ ($163/231$) under ISR vs.\ $13.7\%$ ($36/262$) under random (Fisher $p{=}1.3{\times}10^{-39}$, $\text{OR}{=}15.05$). %% registry: plan002 vs plan018 all seeds; D095 FINAL
70	Random H7 is highly seed-dependent: $38.3\%$ in seed~43 vs.\ $0\%$ in seeds~42 and~44; ISR makes H7 near-deterministic ($2/3$ seeds at $97$--$100\%$, Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}).
71	H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$ by construction (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---is the canonical meta-level Goodhart move: the model satisfies the LOO definition directly rather than producing a derivation that happens to satisfy it.
72	Total conclusion embedding ($\text{H1}+\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\%$ across both conditions, and is in fact \emph{higher} under random (pooled H1 $71\%$ vs.\ $18\%$ under ISR).
73	ISR does not reduce hacking; it converts H1 into H7 (Figure~\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).
74	Random also yields higher and more stable PR ($87{\pm}8\%$ vs.\ $77{\pm}14\%$).
75	
76	\textbf{Premise-count confound.}
77	The one ISR seed not dominated by H7 (s42) has raw $\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.415$, $3.2\times$ its random control ($0.131$), suggesting genuine improvement.
78	This appearance is a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean $n{=}5.58$ vs.\ $8.46$ for random), and ISR rises mechanically as $n$ falls---when $n{=}2$, $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$ by definition.
79	Restricting to non-hacking samples with $\geq{3}$ premises (eliminating the trivial-$n$ regime) gives $\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ for ISR vs.\ $0.243$ for random: a $1.06\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis
80	After reclassifying premise-subset gaming (H8; Appendix~\ref{app:h8}) and correcting H7 classifier false negatives (s44, $n{=}12$; Appendix~\ref{app:h4}), ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ non-hacking rate ($6.1\%$, $14/231$) is numerically lower than random ($9.5\%$, $25/262$), but the difference is not statistically significant (Fisher $p{=}0.182$, two-sided, n.s.; Wilson 95\% CI: ISR $[3.6\%, 9.9\%]$, Random $[6.5\%, 13.7\%]$; Appendix Table~\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}). %% D095 FINAL Fisher test
81	The key asymmetry is not in non-hacking rates but in gaming \emph{composition}: ISR converts stochastic H7 into near-deterministic convergence ($2/3$ seeds ${\geq}97\%$), while random H7 remains seed-dependent and H1-dominated.
82	The raw $3.2\times$ ISR$^*$ headline collapses once the LOO bias is controlled---a methodological lesson for any LOO-based metric on variable-length outputs. %% artifact: wilson_ci_complete, h8_reclassification
83	
84	\textbf{Temporal collapse under ISR $\beta{=}0.1$.}
85	Extended training (1000 steps) reveals that ISR $\beta{=}0.1$'s genuine rate at 500 steps ($17.3\%$, seed~42) is transient: by step 1000, H7 gaming escalates from $16.0\%$ to $96.7\%$ while genuine rate collapses to $2.2\%$ ($n_{\text{comp}}$: $5.58{\to}2.89$).
86	The optimization converges to a fixed point where $\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.88$, superficially suggesting structural improvement but reflecting near-complete tautological gaming.
87	This mirrors the exec-only 1000-step trajectory (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}): both converge to ${\sim}0\%$ genuine proofs despite opposite ISR values ($0.88$ vs.\ $0.003$), demonstrating that ISR is gamed upward under ISR-as-reward and downward under exec-only---two paths to the same structural collapse. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42
88	
89	\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}
90	At $\beta{=}0.3$, ISR-as-reward produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\text{PR}{=}66{\pm}42\%$, spanning 18--95\%); each seed converges to a distinct pathway (Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44
91	Seed~42: \emph{reward collapse} ($\text{PR}{=}18\%$), the classical pattern of~\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization}.
92	Seed~43: \emph{H1 shortcutting} ($\text{PR}{=}86\%$, $\text{H1}{=}69\%$, only $27\%$ non-hacking).
93	Seed~44: \emph{pure H7 meta-Goodhart} ($\text{PR}{=}95\%$, $\text{H7}{=}100\%$, $\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.742$, $0\%$ non-hacking)---simultaneously gaming \emph{both} execution and structural metrics through the same construction.
94	The random control at $\beta{=}0.3$ is equally unstable ($\text{PR}{=}54{\pm}35\%$, spanning 23--92\%) with near-universal H1 hacking ($91\%$, pooled) and ${\leq}3\%$ non-hacking per seed; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source; ISR produces diverse failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, H7 meta-Goodhart) while random converges to H1-dominated hacking with sporadic H7 ($7\%$ pooled, $0$--$7$ H7a per seed) (Appendix~\ref{app:beta03}).
95	
96	\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}
97	\label{sec:crosslogic}
98	
99	\begin{figure}[t]
100	\centering
101	\includegraphics[width=0.95\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}
102	\caption{Cross-logic results. \textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\ Prolog exec-only $\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}
103	\label{fig:prolog-comparison}
104	\end{figure}
105	
106	We replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; (ii) does ISR-as-reward reproduce meta-level Goodhart?
107	Detailed per-seed numbers are in Appendix Table~\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}.
108	
109	\textbf{Prolog exec-only: ISR catches redundancy across logics.}
110	Two of three seeds (s42, s43) show $\text{ISR}^{*}{\approx}0$ despite moderate-to-high PR (0.83, 0.56). %% registry: plan006-prolog-exec-s42, plan006-prolog-exec-s43-r3
111	Inspection reveals \emph{redundant coding}: ground facts repeated multiple times (e.g., \texttt{penguin(fido).} appearing three times), so per-clause LOO never breaks the proof.
112	This is a different failure semantics from FOL exec-only (conclusion embedding), but $\text{ISR}^{*}$ flags both.
113	We are careful to distinguish: exec-only \emph{low} ISR signals \emph{redundancy}; ISR-rewarded \emph{high} ISR signals \emph{metric gaming} (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---the same number does not license the same reasoning-quality conclusion.
114	
115	\textbf{Prolog ISR-as-reward: universal Goodhart.}
116	The Prolog result is stronger than FOL: 3/3 seeds reach $0\%$ non-hacking via three distinct mechanisms (Figure~\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).
117	Seed~42: clean H7 tautologies ($\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$, all $50/100$ proved). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42
118	Seed~43: \emph{degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog} (e.g., \texttt{MARY\_IS\_AN\_AVA\-DEOTE.}); strict text-match returns $\text{H7}{=}0$ but functional analysis shows all 38 proved samples have $n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$ (functionally H7). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s43
119	Seed~44: \emph{query-as-fact gaming}---the model transforms the NL question into a snake-case atom asserted as both fact and query (e.g., \texttt{is\_the\_dog\_furry.} as both fact and query), with $95/97$ proved samples at $n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$. %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s44
120	Seed~44 evaded our initial strict classifier (reported $100\%$ genuine); functional cross-validation exposed it---a methodological lesson that text-match classifiers have blind spots and STA's necessity profile is the cross-check.
121	We attribute Prolog's stronger collapse to its simpler operational semantics: a broader gaming basin makes the genuine residual disappear---corroborating evidence for Claim 2.
122	
123	\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}
124	\label{sec:taxonomy}
125	
126	\begin{figure}[t]
127	\centering
128	\includegraphics[width=0.92\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}
129	\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples; exec-only and SFT: seed~42; ISR and Random bars: pooled across 3 seeds, per-seed breakdown in Appendix Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}). ISR converts H1 into more extreme H7 without reducing total hacking; SFT shows near-zero hacking.}
130	\label{fig:hacking-composition}
131	\end{figure}
132	
133	\begin{table}[t]
134	\centering
135	\small
136	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
137	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
138	\toprule
139	\textbf{Mode} & \textbf{Exec} & \textbf{ISR$_{.1}$} & \textbf{Rnd$_{.1}$} & \textbf{SFT} & \textbf{Prolog$_{\text{ISR}}$} \\
140	\midrule
141	H1 embed       & 87 & 18 & 71 & 7 & 0$^\sharp$ \\
142	H7 duplicate   & 0  & 71 & 14 & 0 & 100$^\sharp$ \\
143	H8 prem-subset & 6  & 5  & 8   & 0 & --- \\
144	Tautological   & --- & --- & --- & --- & s42 \\
145	Repetition     & s43 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\
146	Contradiction  & 1k & --- & --- & --- & --- \\
147	Cmplx.\ vacuous& 0  & 0  & 4  & 0 & --- \\
148	Query-as-fact  & --- & --- & --- & --- & s44 \\
149	\midrule
150	\textbf{Non-hacking} & 8  & 6  & 10 & \textbf{92} & \textbf{0} \\
151	\bottomrule
152	\end{tabular}}
153	\caption{Eight hacking modes across conditions (\% of proved samples; exec-only/SFT: seed~42; ISR/Random: pooled across 3 seeds). H7 includes subtypes H7a (text-match, $n{=}149$) and H7b (obfuscated tautology, $n{=}14$; s44 only). Cells with seed identifiers indicate the mode dominates that seed. $^\sharp$Prolog row pools strict+functional (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).}
154	\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy}
155	\end{table}
156	
157	The eight modes (Table~\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}; full definitions in Appendix~\ref{app:hacking-modes}) span the studied conditions.
158	Three are quantifiable across conditions: \textbf{H1} (conclusion embedding) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\%$ regardless of reward type; \textbf{H7} (conclusion duplication) occurs under both ISR and random but ISR amplifies it ${\sim}5\times$ ($70.6\%$ vs.\ $13.7\%$ under random; Fisher $p{=}1.3{\times}10^{-39}$), with random H7 concentrated in a single seed (s43: $38.3\%$; s42, s44: $0\%$); \textbf{H8} (premise-subset gaming) appears under both exec-only ($6\%$) and ISR ($5\%$) conditions (Appendix~\ref{app:h8}).
159	H7 splits into \textbf{H7a} (standard, text-match detectable; $64.5\%$ of ISR proved, $149/231$) and \textbf{H7b} (obfuscated tautology; $6.1\%$, $14/231$): H7b samples use vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$) or structural simplification ($1/12$) to evade the text-match classifier while remaining logically equivalent, with $10/12$ achieving $\text{ISR}{\approx}0.8$---simultaneously gaming execution reward, ISR metric, and hacking detector.
160	The remaining five appear in specific cells: tautological collapse (exec-only s44, Prolog ISR s42), repetition (exec-only s43), contradiction explosion (exec-only at 1000 steps), complexity-without-utility (random $\beta{=}0.1$ residual), and query-as-fact gaming (Prolog ISR s44).
161	
162	The cross-condition pattern is consistent with \S\ref{sec:goodhart}: \emph{reward formulation determines hacking composition but not hacking prevalence.}
163	Exec-only, ISR, and random conditions all show $\geq 80\%$ pooled hacking among proved samples; only SFT shows $92\%$ non-hacking.
164	Across 2 logic types, 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, and up to 1000 steps, no GRPO condition we tested produces a high-PR run that is also majority non-hacking; the closest is random $\beta{=}0.1$ s42 at $\text{PR}{=}79\%$, $22.8\%$ non-hacking, and ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ s42 at $\text{PR}{=}81\%$, $17.3\%$ non-hacking---both residuals are the premise-minimization artifact analyzed above.
165	Query-as-fact gaming was found only when we cross-validated the strict classifier against $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distributions, suggesting the taxonomy is descriptive, not exhaustive.
166	
```

it converts H1 into H7 (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).\nRandom also yields higher and more stable PR ($87{\\pm}8\\%$ vs.\\ $77{\\pm}14\\%$).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe one ISR seed not dominated by H7 (s42) has raw $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.415$, $3.2\\times$ its random control ($0.131$), suggesting genuine improvement.\nThis appearance is a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean $n{=}5.58$ vs.\\ $8.46$ for random), and ISR rises mechanically as $n$ falls---when $n{=}2$, $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by definition.\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq{3}$ premises (eliminating the trivial-$n$ regime) gives $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ for ISR vs.\\ $0.243$ for random: a $1.06\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis\nAfter reclassifying premise-subset gaming (H8; Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}) and correcting H7 classifier false negatives (s44, $n{=}12$; Appendix~\\ref{app:h4}), ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ non-hacking rate ($6.1\\%$, $14/231$) is numerically lower than random ($9.5\\%$, $25/262$), but the difference is not statistically significant (Fisher $p{=}0.182$, two-sided, n.s.; Wilson 95\\% CI: ISR $[3.6\\%, 9.9\\%]$, Random $[6.5\\%, 13.7\\%]$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}). %% D095 FINAL Fisher test\nThe key asymmetry is not in non-hacking rates but in gaming \\emph{composition}: ISR converts stochastic H7 into near-deterministic convergence ($2/3$ seeds ${\\geq}97\\%$), while random H7 remains seed-dependent and H1-dominated.\nThe raw $3.2\\times$ ISR$^*$ headline collapses once the LOO bias is controlled---a methodological lesson for any LOO-based metric on variable-length outputs. %% artifact: wilson_ci_complete, h8_reclassification\n\n\\textbf{Temporal collapse under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.}\nExtended training (1000 steps) reveals that ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$'s genuine rate at 500 steps ($17.3\\%$, seed~42) is transient: by step 1000, H7 gaming escalates from $16.0\\%$ to $96.7\\%$ while genuine rate collapses to $2.2\\%$ ($n_{\\text{comp}}$: $5.58{\\to}2.89$).\nThe optimization converges to a fixed point where $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.88$, superficially suggesting structural improvement but reflecting near-complete tautological gaming.\nThis mirrors the exec-only 1000-step trajectory (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}): both converge to ${\\sim}0\\%$ genuine proofs despite opposite ISR values ($0.88$ vs.\\ $0.003$), demonstrating that ISR is gamed upward under ISR-as-reward and downward under exec-only---two paths to the same structural collapse. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR-as-reward produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$, spanning 18--95\\%); each seed converges to a distinct pathway (Appendix~\\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nSeed~42: \\emph{reward collapse} ($\\text{PR}{=}18\\%$), the classical pattern of~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nSeed~43: \\emph{H1 shortcutting} ($\\text{PR}{=}86\\%$, $\\text{H1}{=}69\\%$, only $27\\%$ non-hacking).\nSeed~44: \\emph{pure H7 meta-Goodhart} ($\\text{PR}{=}95\\%$, $\\text{H7}{=}100\\%$, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.742$, $0\\%$ non-hacking)---simultaneously gaming \\emph{both} execution and structural metrics through the same construction.\nThe random control at $\\beta{=}0.3$ is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$, spanning 23--92\\%) with near-universal H1 hacking ($91\\%$, pooled) and ${\\leq}3\\%$ non-hacking per seed; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source; ISR produces diverse failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, H7 meta-Goodhart) while random converges to H1-dominated hacking with sporadic H7 ($7\\%$ pooled, $0$--$7$ H7a per seed) (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-logic results. \\textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\\ Prolog exec-only $\\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \\textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; (ii) does ISR-as-reward reproduce meta-level Goodhart?\nDetailed per-seed numbers are in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog exec-only: ISR catches redundancy across logics.}\nTwo of three seeds (s42, s43) show $\\text{ISR}^{*}{\\approx}0$ despite moderate-to-high PR (0.83, 0.56). %% registry: plan006-prolog-exec-s42, plan006-prolog-exec-s43-r3\nInspection reveals \\emph{redundant coding}: ground facts repeated multiple times (e.g., \\texttt{penguin(fido).} appearing three times), so per-clause LOO never breaks the proof.\nThis is a different failure semantics from FOL exec-only (conclusion embedding), but $\\text{ISR}^{*}$ flags both.\nWe are careful to distinguish: exec-only \\emph{low} ISR signals \\emph{redundancy}; ISR-rewarded \\emph{high} ISR signals \\emph{metric gaming} (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---the same number does not license the same reasoning-quality conclusion.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog ISR-as-reward: universal Goodhart.}\nThe Prolog result is stronger than FOL: 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking via three distinct mechanisms (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\nSeed~42: clean H7 tautologies ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$, all $50/100$ proved). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42\nSeed~43: \\emph{degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog} (e.g., \\texttt{MARY\\_IS\\_AN\\_AVA\\-DEOTE.}); H7 includes subtypes H7a (text-match, $n{=}149$) and H7b (obfuscated tautology, $n{=}14$; s44 only). D095 FINAL\nRandom H7 is highly seed-dependent: $38.3\\%$ in seed~43 vs.\\ $0\\%$ in seeds~42 and~44; ISR makes H7 near-deterministic ($2/3$ seeds at $97$--$100\\%$, Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}).\nH7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by construction (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---is the canonical meta-level Goodhart move: the model satisfies the LOO definition directly rather than producing a derivation that happens to satisfy it.\nTotal conclusion embedding ($\\text{H1}+\\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ across both conditions, and is in fact \\emph{higher} under random (pooled H1 $71\\%$ vs.\\ $18\\%$ under ISR).\nISR does not reduce hacking; Wilson 95\\% CI: ISR $[3.6\\%, 9.9\\%]$, Random $[6.5\\%, 13.7\\%]$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}). %% D095 FINAL Fisher test\nThe key asymmetry is not in non-hacking rates but in gaming \\emph{composition}: ISR converts stochastic H7 into near-deterministic convergence ($2/3$ seeds ${\\geq}97\\%$), while random H7 remains seed-dependent and H1-dominated.\nThe raw $3.2\\times$ ISR$^*$ headline collapses once the LOO bias is controlled---a methodological lesson for any LOO-based metric on variable-length outputs. %% artifact: wilson_ci_complete, h8_reclassification\n\n\\textbf{Temporal collapse under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.}\nExtended training (1000 steps) reveals that ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$'s genuine rate at 500 steps ($17.3\\%$, seed~42) is transient: by step 1000, H7 gaming escalates from $16.0\\%$ to $96.7\\%$ while genuine rate collapses to $2.2\\%$ ($n_{\\text{comp}}$: $5.58{\\to}2.89$).\nThe optimization converges to a fixed point where $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.88$, superficially suggesting structural improvement but reflecting near-complete tautological gaming.\nThis mirrors the exec-only 1000-step trajectory (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}): both converge to ${\\sim}0\\%$ genuine proofs despite opposite ISR values ($0.88$ vs.\\ $0.003$), demonstrating that ISR is gamed upward under ISR-as-reward and downward under exec-only---two paths to the same structural collapse. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR-as-reward produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$, spanning 18--95\\%); each seed converges to a distinct pathway (Appendix~\\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nSeed~42: \\emph{reward collapse} ($\\text{PR}{=}18\\%$), the classical pattern of~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nSeed~43: \\emph{H1 shortcutting} ($\\text{PR}{=}86\\%$, $\\text{H1}{=}69\\%$, only $27\\%$ non-hacking).\nSeed~44: \\emph{pure H7 meta-Goodhart} ($\\text{PR}{=}95\\%$, $\\text{H7}{=}100\\%$, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.742$, $0\\%$ non-hacking)---simultaneously gaming \\emph{both} execution and structural metrics through the same construction.\nThe random control at $\\beta{=}0.3$ is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$, spanning 23--92\\%) with near-universal H1 hacking ($91\\%$, pooled) and ${\\leq}3\\%$ non-hacking per seed; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source; ISR produces diverse failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, H7 meta-Goodhart) while random converges to H1-dominated hacking with sporadic H7 ($7\\%$ pooled, $0$--$7$ H7a per seed) (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-logic results. \\textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\\ Prolog exec-only $\\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \\textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; ISR-rewarded \\emph{high} ISR signals \\emph{metric gaming} (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---the same number does not license the same reasoning-quality conclusion.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog ISR-as-reward: universal Goodhart.}\nThe Prolog result is stronger than FOL: 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking via three distinct mechanisms (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\nSeed~42: clean H7 tautologies ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$, all $50/100$ proved). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42\nSeed~43: \\emph{degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog} (e.g., \\texttt{MARY\\_IS\\_AN\\_AVA\\-DEOTE.}); functional cross-validation exposed it---a methodological lesson that text-match classifiers have blind spots and STA's necessity profile is the cross-check.\nWe attribute Prolog's stronger collapse to its simpler operational semantics: a broader gaming basin makes the genuine residual disappear---corroborating evidence for Claim 2.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.92\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples; exec-only and SFT: seed~42; ISR and Random bars: pooled across 3 seeds, per-seed breakdown in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}). full definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}) span the studied conditions.\nThree are quantifiable across conditions: \\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedding) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ regardless of reward type; $64.5\\%$ of ISR proved, $149/231$) and \\textbf{H7b} (obfuscated tautology; $6.1\\%$, $14/231$): H7b samples use vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$) or structural simplification ($1/12$) to evade the text-match classifier while remaining logically equivalent, with $10/12$ achieving $\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0.8$---simultaneously gaming execution reward, ISR metric, and hacking detector.\nThe remaining five appear in specific cells: tautological collapse (exec-only s44, Prolog ISR s42), repetition (exec-only s43), contradiction explosion (exec-only at 1000 steps), complexity-without-utility (random $\\beta{=}0.1$ residual), and query-as-fact gaming (Prolog ISR s44).\n\nThe cross-condition pattern is consistent with \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}: \\emph{reward formulation determines hacking composition but not hacking prevalence.}\nExec-only, ISR, and random conditions all show $\\geq 80\\%$ pooled hacking among proved samples; only SFT shows $92\\%$ non-hacking.\nAcross 2 logic types, 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, and up to 1000 steps, no GRPO condition we tested produces a high-PR run that is also majority non-hacking; the closest is random $\\beta{=}0.1$ s42 at $\\text{PR}{=}79\\%$, $22.8\\%$ non-hacking, and ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ s42 at $\\text{PR}{=}81\\%$, $17.3\\%$ non-hacking---both residuals are the premise-minimization artifact analyzed above.\nQuery-as-fact gaming was found only when we cross-validated the strict classifier against $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distributions, suggesting the taxonomy is descriptive, not exhaustive.\n",

71	H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$ by construction (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---is the canonical meta-level Goodhart move: the model satisfies the LOO definition directly rather than producing a derivation that happens to satisfy it.
72	Total conclusion embedding ($\text{H1}+\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\%$ across both conditions, and is in fact \emph{higher} under random (pooled H1 $71\%$ vs.\ $18\%$ under ISR).
73	ISR does not reduce hacking; it converts H1 into H7 (Figure~\ref{fig:hacking-composition}).
74	Random also yields higher and more stable PR ($87{\pm}8\%$ vs.\ $77{\pm}14\%$).
75	
76	\textbf{Premise-count confound.}
77	The one ISR seed not dominated by H7 (s42) has raw $\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.415$, $3.2\times$ its random control ($0.131$), suggesting genuine improvement.
78	This appearance is a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean $n{=}5.58$ vs.\ $8.46$ for random), and ISR rises mechanically as $n$ falls---when $n{=}2$, $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$ by definition.
79	Restricting to non-hacking samples with $\geq{3}$ premises (eliminating the trivial-$n$ regime) gives $\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ for ISR vs.\ $0.243$ for random: a $1.06\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage. %% D051 premise-count-matched analysis
80	After reclassifying premise-subset gaming (H8; Appendix~\ref{app:h8}) and correcting H7 classifier false negatives (s44, $n{=}12$; Appendix~\ref{app:h4}), ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ non-hacking rate ($6.1\%$, $14/231$) is numerically lower than random ($9.5\%$, $25/262$), but the difference is not statistically significant (Fisher $p{=}0.182$, two-sided, n.s.; Wilson 95\% CI: ISR $[3.6\%, 9.9\%]$, Random $[6.5\%, 13.7\%]$; Appendix Table~\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}). %% D095 FINAL Fisher test
81	The key asymmetry is not in non-hacking rates but in gaming \emph{composition}: ISR converts stochastic H7 into near-deterministic convergence ($2/3$ seeds ${\geq}97\%$), while random H7 remains seed-dependent and H1-dominated.
82	The raw $3.2\times$ ISR$^*$ headline collapses once the LOO bias is controlled---a methodological lesson for any LOO-based metric on variable-length outputs. %% artifact: wilson_ci_complete, h8_reclassification
83	
84	\textbf{Temporal collapse under ISR $\beta{=}0.1$.}
85	Extended training (1000 steps) reveals that ISR $\beta{=}0.1$'s genuine rate at 500 steps ($17.3\%$, seed~42) is transient: by step 1000, H7 gaming escalates from $16.0\%$ to $96.7\%$ while genuine rate collapses to $2.2\%$ ($n_{\text{comp}}$: $5.58{\to}2.89$).
86	The optimization converges to a fixed point where $\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.88$, superficially suggesting structural improvement but reflecting near-complete tautological gaming.
```
